"""
Demonstrates the Kitty keyboard protocol support in Textual.

When run in a terminal that supports the Kitty keyboard protocol (such as
Kitty, WezTerm, or foot), key presses, repeats and releases are reported with
their full metadata. Otherwise Textual falls back to the traditional escape
sequence handling.

Press `q` or `ctrl+q` to quit.
"""

from textual.app import App, ComposeResult
from textual.events import Key
from textual.widgets import Footer, RichLog


class KittyKeyboardProtocolApp(App):
    """An app that displays Kitty keyboard protocol key events."""

    BINDINGS = [("q", "quit", "Quit")]

    def compose(self) -> ComposeResult:
        yield RichLog(id="events", wrap=True, highlight=True)
        yield Footer()

    def on_key(self, event: Key) -> None:
        """Write details of every key event to the log."""
        self.query_one("#events", RichLog).write(
            f"phase={event.phase} character={event.character!r} "
            f"key={event.key!r} modifiers={event.modifiers} "
            f"base_key={event.base_key!r} shifted_key={event.shifted_key!r} "
            f"base_layout_key={event.base_layout_key!r}"
        )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    KittyKeyboardProtocolApp().run()

"""
Demonstrates the Kitty keyboard protocol support in Textual.

When run in a terminal that supports the Kitty keyboard protocol (such as
Kitty, WezTerm, or foot), key presses, repeats and releases are reported with
their full metadata. Otherwise Textual falls back to the traditional escape
sequence handling.

Press `q` or `ctrl+q` to quit.
"""

from textual.app import App, ComposeResult
from textual.events import Key
from textual.widgets import Footer, RichLog


class KittyKeyboardProtocolApp(App):
    """An app that displays Kitty keyboard protocol key events."""

    BINDINGS = [("q", "quit", "Quit")]

    def compose(self) -> ComposeResult:
        yield RichLog(id="events", wrap=True, highlight=True)
        yield Footer()

    def on_key(self, event: Key) -> None:
        """Write details of every key event to the log."""
        self.query_one("#events", RichLog).write(
            f"phase={event.phase} character={event.character!r} "
            f"key={event.key!r} modifiers={event.modifiers} "
            f"base_key={event.base_key!r} shifted_key={event.shifted_key!r} "
            f"base_layout_key={event.base_layout_key!r}"
        )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    KittyKeyboardProtocolApp().run()

cd /app && cat >> tests/test_xterm_parser.py <<'PY'


def test_kitty_key_phase_and_metadata(parser):
    """Kitty keyboard protocol sequences expose phase and stable metadata."""
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[97;3u\x1b[97;3:2u\x1b[97;3:3u"))

    assert [event.phase for event in events] == ["press", "repeat", "release"]
    assert all(event.key == "alt+a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.modifiers == ("alt",) for event in events)
    assert all(event.base_key == "a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.shifted_key == "a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.base_layout_key == "a" for event in events)

    assert events[0].is_press
    assert events[1].is_repeat
    assert events[2].is_release


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "sequence,key,character,modifiers,base_key,shifted_key",
    [
        # Shift-only printable events preserve the shifted character and base key.
        ("\x1b[97;2u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[65;2u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[97;2;65u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        # Non-shift modified shortcuts keep their public name and no character.
        ("\x1b[97;3u", "alt+a", None, ("alt",), "a", "a"),
        ("\x1b[65;4u", "alt+shift+a", None, ("alt", "shift"), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[120;7u", "alt+ctrl+x", None, ("alt", "ctrl"), "x", "x"),
        # Associated-text-only key code 0 uses its text as both key and character.
        ("\x1b[0;1;65u", "A", "A", (), "A", "A"),
        # Alternate keys use Textual shifted names.
        ("\x1b[61;2u", "plus", "+", ("shift",), "equals_sign", "plus"),
        ("\x1b[61;6u", "ctrl+plus", None, ("ctrl", "shift"), "equals_sign", "plus"),
        ("\x1b[43;2u", "plus", "+", ("shift",), "equals_sign", "plus"),
        # Digits keep the base key name in the public key.
        ("\x1b[49;6u", "ctrl+shift+1", None, ("ctrl", "shift"), "1", "exclamation_mark"),
    ],
)
def test_kitty_key_metadata(
    parser, sequence, key, character, modifiers, base_key, shifted_key
):
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == key
    assert event.character == character
    assert event.modifiers == modifiers
    assert event.base_key == base_key
    assert event.shifted_key == shifted_key
    assert event.base_layout_key == base_key


def test_kitty_alternate_key_alias(parser):
    """Ctrl+Shift+= produces the alternate key name and an unshifted alias."""
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[61;6u"))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == "ctrl+plus"
    assert "ctrl+shift+equals_sign" in event.aliases
    assert event.shift
    assert event.ctrl


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "sequence,key,character,modifiers,base_key",
    [
        ("\x1b ", "alt+space", " ", ("alt",), "space"),
        ("\x1b\r", "alt+enter", "\r", ("alt",), "enter"),
        ("\x1b\x7f", "alt+backspace", "\x7f", ("alt",), "backspace"),
        ("\x1b\x01", "alt+ctrl+a", "\x01", ("alt", "ctrl"), "a"),
        ("\x1ba", "alt+a", "a", ("alt",), "a"),
        ("\x1bA", "alt+shift+a", "A", ("alt", "shift"), "a"),
    ],
)
def test_legacy_alt_prefixed_fallback(
    parser, sequence, key, character, modifiers, base_key
):
    """Legacy ESC-prefixed fallback keeps stable public keys and metadata."""
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    events.extend(parser.feed(""))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == key
    assert event.character == character
    assert event.modifiers == modifiers
    assert event.base_key == base_key

def test_key_event_metadata():
    """Key events expose phase, modifiers, and stable key metadata."""
    from textual.events import Key

    press = Key(
        "alt+ctrl+a",
        None,
        modifiers=["ctrl", "alt"],
        base_key="a",
        shifted_key="A",
        base_layout_key="a",
    )
    assert press.phase == "press"
    assert press.modifiers == ("alt", "ctrl")
    assert press.base_key == "a"
    assert press.shifted_key == "A"
    assert press.base_layout_key == "a"
    assert press.is_press
    assert not press.is_repeat
    assert not press.is_release
    assert press.alt
    assert press.ctrl
    assert not press.shift
    assert not press.super
    assert not press.hyper
    assert not press.meta

    repeat = press.copy()
    repeat.phase = "repeat"
    assert repeat.is_repeat
    release = press.copy()
    release.phase = "release"
    assert release.is_release


def test_key_aliases_for_shifted_keys():
    """Aliases include both shifted and unshifted forms of alternate keys."""
    from textual.events import Key

    event = Key("ctrl+plus", None)
    assert "ctrl+plus" in event.aliases
    assert "ctrl+shift+equals_sign" in event.aliases

    event = Key("plus", "+")
    assert "plus" in event.aliases
    assert "shift+equals_sign" in event.aliases

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
path = "src/textual/keys.py"
text = open(path).read()
old = '''def _get_key_aliases(key: str) -> list[str]:
    """Return all aliases for the given key, including the key itself."""
    aliases = [key] + list(KEY_ALIASES.get(key, []))
    if "+" in key:
        modifiers, key_name = key.rpartition("+")[::2]
    else:
        modifiers, key_name = "", key
    base_key = SHIFTED_KEY_NAMES.get(key_name)
    if base_key is not None and "shift" not in modifiers.split("+"):
        # A shifted key (e.g. "plus") implies the shift modifier was held.
        # Add an alias using the unshifted base key so handlers declared with
        # either the shifted or unshifted form both match.
        if modifiers:
            aliases.append(f"{modifiers}+shift+{base_key}")
        else:
            aliases.append(f"shift+{base_key}")
    return aliases
'''
new = '''def _get_key_aliases(key: str) -> list[str]:
    """Return all aliases for the given key, including the key itself."""
    aliases = [key] + list(KEY_ALIASES.get(key, []))
    if "+" in key:
        modifiers, key_name = key.rpartition("+")[::2]
    else:
        modifiers, key_name = "", key
    modifier_tokens = modifiers.split("+") if modifiers else []
    base_key = SHIFTED_KEY_NAMES.get(key_name)
    if base_key is not None and "shift" not in modifier_tokens:
        # The key name is a shifted form (e.g. "plus") which implies the shift
        # modifier was held. Add an alias using the unshifted base key so that
        # handlers/bindings declared with either form match.
        if modifiers:
            aliases.append(f"{modifiers}+shift+{base_key}")
        else:
            aliases.append(f"shift+{base_key}")
    else:
        shifted_key = BASE_TO_SHIFTED_KEYS.get(key_name)
        if shifted_key is not None and "shift" in modifier_tokens:
            # The key name is an unshifted base key (e.g. "equals_sign") with
            # shift held. Add an alias using the shifted form, dropping the
            # implicit shift modifier.
            shifted_modifiers = "+".join(
                token for token in modifier_tokens if token != "shift"
            )
            aliases.append(
                f"{shifted_modifiers}+{shifted_key}" if shifted_modifiers else shifted_key
            )
    return aliases
'''
assert old in text
text = text.replace(old, new, 1)
open(path, "w").write(text)
print("done")

            public_key_name = shifted_key
        else:
            public_modifiers = modifiers

        """Handle a key press.

        This method is used internally by the bindings system.

        Args:
            key: A key.
            priority: If `True` check from `App` down, otherwise from focused up.

        Returns:
            True if the key was handled by a binding, otherwise False
        """
        for namespace, bindings in (
            reversed(self.screen._binding_chain)
            if priority
            else self.screen._modal_binding_chain
        ):
            key_bindings = bindings.key_to_bindings.get(key, ())
            for binding in key_bindings:
                if binding.priority == priority:
                    if await self.run_action(binding.action, namespace):
                        return True
        return False
'''
new = '''    async def _check_bindings(
        self,
        key: str,
        priority: bool = False,
        aliases: Iterable[str] = (),
    ) -> bool:
        """Handle a key press.

        This method is used internally by the bindings system.

        Args:
            key: A key.
            priority: If `True` check from `App` down, otherwise from focused up.
            aliases: Additional names for the key (e.g. alternate shifted forms).

        Returns:
            True if the key was handled by a binding, otherwise False

            reversed(self.screen._binding_chain)
            if priority
            else self.screen._modal_binding_chain
        ):

                        if await self.run_action(binding.action, namespace):

        return False

import pytest
from textual.app import App
from textual.events import Key


async def test_alternate_key_binding_matches_shifted_form():

async def test_alternate_key_binding_matches_shifted_form(key, aliases):
    """Bindings match both the shifted and unshifted forms of alternate keys."""
    from textual.app import App
    from textual.events import Key

from textual._xterm_parser import XTermParser

def parse(seq):
    p = XTermParser()
    evs = list(p.feed(seq))
    try:
        evs.extend(p.feed(""))
    except Exception:
        pass
    return evs

for seq in ["\x1b[97;3:2u", "\x1b[97;3:3u", "\x1b[97;1;65:2u", "\x1b[0;1;65u"]:
    evs = parse(seq)
    print(repr(seq), "->", [(e.key, e.character, e.phase) for e in evs])

# Change Log

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](http://keepachangelog.com/)
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

## Unreleased

### [7.5.0] - 2026-01-29

- The DataTable row cursor will extend to the full width if there is excess space https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6345
- The DataTable will send a selected event on click, only if the cell / row / column is currently highlighted https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6345

## [7.4.0] - 2026-01-25

### Added

- Added `pointer` rule https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6339

## [7.3.0] - 2026-01-15

### Fixed

- Fixed triple click on command palette raising an exception https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6329

### Added

- Added `DOM.query_one_optional`
- Added `default` parameter to `get_component_rich_style` get_component_rich_style

### Changed

- Added super+c (command on mac) alternative bindings for copy, for terminals that support it (Ghostty does)
- Allow `Sparkline` to be of any height, not just 1 https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6171

## [7.2.0] - 2026-01-11

### Changed


END STEP

path = "CHANGELOG.md"

### [7.5.0] - 2026-01-29
'''
new = '''## Unreleased

### Added

- Extended `Key` events with Kitty keyboard protocol metadata (`phase`, `modifiers`, `base_key`, `shifted_key`, and `base_layout_key`) and convenience properties (`is_press`, `is_repeat`, `is_release`, `shift`, `alt`, `ctrl`, `super`, `hyper`, and `meta`).
- Added `examples/kitty_keyboard_protocol.py`.

### Changed

- Kitty keyboard protocol sequences now distinguish press, repeat, and release events, and preserve stable metadata for text-reporting and alternate keys.
- Alternate-key shortcuts (e.g. `ctrl+plus`) now match their shifted forms through key aliases.
- Legacy ESC-prefixed fallback preserves public key names and metadata for Enter, Space, Backspace, and Ctrl+letter.

### [7.5.0] - 2026-01-29
'''

text = text.replace(old, new, 1)
open(path, "w").write(text)
print("done")

# Change Log

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](http://keepachangelog.com/)
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

## Unreleased

### Added

- Extended `Key` events with Kitty keyboard protocol metadata (`phase`, `modifiers`, `base_key`, `shifted_key`, and `base_layout_key`) and convenience properties (`is_press`, `is_repeat`, `is_release`, `shift`, `alt`, `ctrl`, `super`, `hyper`, and `meta`).
- Added `examples/kitty_keyboard_protocol.py`.

### Changed

- Kitty keyboard protocol sequences now distinguish press, repeat, and release events, and preserve stable metadata for text-reporting and alternate keys.
- Alternate-key shortcuts (e.g. `ctrl+plus`) now match their shifted forms through key aliases.
- Legacy ESC-prefixed fallback preserves public key names and metadata for Enter, Space, Backspace, and Ctrl+letter.

### [7.5.0] - 2026-01-29

- The DataTable row cursor will extend to the full width if there is excess space https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6345
- The DataTable will send a selected event on click, only if the cell / row / column is currently highlighted https://github.com/Textualize/textual/pull/6345

@@ -324,6 +365,229 @@ class XTermParser(Parser[Message]):
             self._debug_log_file.close()
             self._debug_log_file = None
 
+    @staticmethod
+    def _modifiers_from_bits(modifier_bits: int) -> tuple[str, ...]:
+        """Convert a Kitty modifier bitmask to a sorted tuple of modifier names."""
+        return tuple(
+            sorted(
+                name
+                for bit, name in enumerate(_MODIFIER_NAMES)
+                if modifier_bits & (1 << bit)
+            )
+        )
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _parse_colon_codepoints(text_str: str | None) -> list[str | None]:
+        """Convert codepoints split on colons in to a list of characters.
+
+        Args:
+            text_str: String with groups of digits, separated by colons.
+
+        Returns:
+            A list of characters (or `[None]` if no text was supplied).
+        """
+        if not text_str:
+            return [None]
+        return [
+            chr(int(part)) if part.isdecimal() else chr(1)
+            for part in text_str.split(":")
+        ]
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _event_from_key_name(
+        name: str, character: str | None, alt: bool = False
+    ) -> events.Key:
+        """Build a key event from a Textual key name.
+
+        Args:
+            name: A key name, e.g. ``"enter"`` or ``"ctrl+a"``.
+            character: The character produced by the key, if any.
+            alt: If `True`, apply an ``alt`` prefix to the key.
+
+        Returns:
+            A `Key` event with stable metadata.
+        """
+        parts = name.split("+")
+        modifiers = tuple(sorted(parts[:-1])) if len(parts) > 1 else ()
+        base_key = parts[-1]
+        shifted_key = base_key
+        if len(base_key) == 1 and base_key.isupper() and (alt or "shift" in modifiers):
+            # An upper-case letter implies shift was held. Normalise the base
+            # key to its lower-case form.
+            base_key = base_key.lower()
+            if "shift" not in modifiers:
+                modifiers = tuple(sorted((*modifiers, "shift")))
+            shifted_key = base_key.upper()
+        if alt:
+            modifiers = tuple(sorted((*modifiers, "alt")))
+        key = "+".join((*modifiers, base_key))
+        return events.Key(
+            key,
+            character,
+            modifiers=modifiers,
+            base_key=base_key,
+            shifted_key=shifted_key,
+            base_layout_key=base_key,
+        )
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _kitty_key_event(
+        *,
+        number: int,
+        end: str,
+        modifiers: tuple[str, ...],
+        text: str | None,
+        phase: str,
+        functional_key: str,
+    ) -> events.Key:
+        """Create a `Key` event from parsed Kitty keyboard protocol fields.
+
+        Args:
+            number: The key code.
+            end: The terminating character of the sequence.
+            modifiers: The sorted tuple of modifier names.
+            text: The text produced by the key, if reported.
+            phase: ``"press"``, ``"repeat"`` or ``"release"``.
+            functional_key: The Textual key name for a functional key, or empty.
+
+        Returns:
+            A `Key` event with stable metadata.
+        """
+        if functional_key:
+            # A functional / special key (arrows, f-keys, modifier keys, ...).
+            base_key = shifted_key = base_layout_key = functional_key
+            if functional_key in MODIFIER_FUNCTIONAL_KEYS:
+                public_key = functional_key
+                public_modifiers: tuple[str, ...] = ()
+            else:
+                public_modifiers = modifiers
+                public_key = "+".join((*sorted(public_modifiers), functional_key))
+            if text is not None:
+                character = text
+            elif not modifiers:
+                character = SPECIAL_KEY_TO_CHARACTER.get(functional_key)
+            else:
+                character = None
+            return events.Key(
+                public_key,
+                character,
+                phase=phase,
+                modifiers=modifiers,
+                base_key=base_key,
+                shifted_key=shifted_key,
+                base_layout_key=base_layout_key,
+            )
+
+        if number == 0:
+            # Associated-text-only key: use the text as both the key and character.
+            key_name = (
+                _character_to_key(text) if text is not None and len(text) == 1 else ""
+            )
+            if not key_name:
+                key_name = text if text is not None else ""
+            public_key = (
+                "+".join((*sorted(modifiers), key_name)) if modifiers else key_name
+            )
+            return events.Key(
+                public_key,
+                text,
+                phase=phase,
+                modifiers=modifiers,
+                base_key=key_name,
+                shifted_key=key_name,
+                base_layout_key=key_name,
+            )
+
+        base_character = chr(number)
+        if "shift" in modifiers:
+            # Normalise an alternate key code (e.g. 'A' or '+') back to the
+            # unshifted base key.
+            if base_character.isupper():
+                base_character = base_character.lower()
+            else:
+                for base_char, shifted_char in _SHIFTED_CHARACTERS.items():
+                    if base_character == shifted_char:
+                        base_character = base_char
+                        break
+        base_key = _character_to_key(base_character)
+        base_layout_key = base_key
+
+        if text is not None:
+            shifted_character = text
+        elif "shift" in modifiers:
+            if base_character.isalpha():
+                shifted_character = base_character.upper()
+            else:
+                shifted_character = _SHIFTED_CHARACTERS.get(
+                    base_character, base_character
+                )
+        else:
+            shifted_character = base_character
+
+        shifted_key = (
+            _character_to_key(shifted_character)
+            if len(shifted_character) == 1
+            else shifted_character
+        )
+
+        non_shift_modifiers = tuple(
+            modifier for modifier in modifiers if modifier != "shift"
+        )
+        if text is not None:
+            character = text if not non_shift_modifiers else None
+        else:
+            character = shifted_character if not non_shift_modifiers else None
+
+        # The public key keeps the stable unshifted base name. Alternate keys
+        # (e.g. Shift+= produces "+") are available through `shifted_key` and
+        # as aliases, so both the shifted and unshifted forms can be bound.
+        public_key = "+".join((*sorted(modifiers), base_key))
+        return events.Key(
+            public_key,
+            character,
+            phase=phase,
+            modifiers=modifiers,
+            base_key=base_key,
+            shifted_key=shifted_key,

END STEP

+    @classmethod
+    @lru_cache(maxsize=1024)
+    def _parse_extended_key(cls, sequence: str) -> list[events.Key] | None:
+        """Parse a Kitty keyboard protocol sequence.
+
+        Args:
+            sequence: The input sequence.
+
+        Returns:
+            A list of key events, or `None` if the sequence could not be parsed.
+        """
+        if (match := _re_extended_key.fullmatch(sequence)) is None:
+            return None
+
+        number = int(match["number"])
+        modifier_bits = int(match["modifiers"] or 1) - 1
+        event_type = int(match["event_type"] or 1)
+        text_str = match["text"]
+        end = match["end"]
+
+        phase = _PHASES.get(event_type, "press")
+        modifiers = cls._modifiers_from_bits(modifier_bits)
+        functional_key = FUNCTIONAL_KEYS.get(f"{number}{end}", "")
+
+        return [
+            cls._kitty_key_event(
+                number=number,
+                end=end,
+                modifiers=modifiers,
+                text=text,
+                phase=phase,
+                functional_key=functional_key,
+            )
+            for text in cls._parse_colon_codepoints(text_str)
+        ]

+# Alternate key names for keys which produce a different character when shifted.
+# The keys of this mapping are the Textual names of the shifted characters and
+# the values are the Textual names of the unshifted (base) keys. For example,
+# on a US layout pressing Shift+= produces "+", so "plus" is the shifted form

def test_mouse_event_detected_but_info_not_parsed(parser):
    # I don't know if this can actually happen in reality, but
    # there's a branch in the code that allows for the possibility.
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[<65;18;20;25M"))
    assert len(events) == 0


@pytest.mark.xfail()
def test_escape_sequence_resulting_in_multiple_keypresses(parser):
    """Some sequences are interpreted as more than 1 keypress"""
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[2;4~"))
    assert len(events) == 2
    assert events[0].key == "escape"
    assert events[1].key == "shift+insert"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("parameter", range(1, 5))
def test_terminal_mode_reporting_synchronized_output_supported(parser, parameter):
    sequence = f"\x1b[?2026;{parameter}$y"
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    assert len(events) == 1
    assert isinstance(events[0], TerminalSupportsSynchronizedOutput)


def test_terminal_mode_reporting_synchronized_output_not_supported(parser):
    sequence = "\x1b[?2026;0$y"
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    assert events == []


def test_kitty_key_phase_and_metadata(parser):
    """Kitty keyboard protocol sequences expose phase and stable metadata."""
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[97;3u\x1b[97;3:2u\x1b[97;3:3u"))

    assert [event.phase for event in events] == ["press", "repeat", "release"]
    assert all(event.key == "alt+a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.modifiers == ("alt",) for event in events)
    assert all(event.base_key == "a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.shifted_key == "a" for event in events)
    assert all(event.base_layout_key == "a" for event in events)

    assert events[0].is_press
    assert events[1].is_repeat
    assert events[2].is_release


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "sequence,key,character,modifiers,base_key,shifted_key",
    [
        # Shift-only printable events preserve the shifted character and base key.
        ("\x1b[97;2u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[65;2u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[97;2;65u", "shift+a", "A", ("shift",), "a", "A"),
        # Non-shift modified shortcuts keep their public name and no character.
        ("\x1b[97;3u", "alt+a", None, ("alt",), "a", "a"),
        ("\x1b[65;4u", "alt+shift+a", None, ("alt", "shift"), "a", "A"),
        ("\x1b[120;7u", "alt+ctrl+x", None, ("alt", "ctrl"), "x", "x"),
        # Associated-text-only key code 0 uses its text as both key and character.
        ("\x1b[0;1;65u", "A", "A", (), "A", "A"),
        # Alternate keys use Textual shifted names.
        ("\x1b[61;2u", "shift+equals_sign", "+", ("shift",), "equals_sign", "plus"),
        (
            "\x1b[61;6u",
            "ctrl+shift+equals_sign",
            None,
            ("ctrl", "shift"),
            "equals_sign",
            "plus",
        ),
        ("\x1b[43;2u", "shift+equals_sign", "+", ("shift",), "equals_sign", "plus"),
        # Digits keep the base key name in the public key.
        (
            "\x1b[49;6u",
            "ctrl+shift+1",
            None,
            ("ctrl", "shift"),
            "1",
            "exclamation_mark",
        ),
    ],
)
def test_kitty_key_metadata(
    parser, sequence, key, character, modifiers, base_key, shifted_key
):
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == key
    assert event.character == character
    assert event.modifiers == modifiers
    assert event.base_key == base_key
    assert event.shifted_key == shifted_key
    assert event.base_layout_key == base_key


def test_kitty_alternate_key_alias(parser):
    """Ctrl+Shift+= keeps its stable public key and exposes a shifted alias."""
    events = list(parser.feed("\x1b[61;6u"))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == "ctrl+shift+equals_sign"
    assert "ctrl+plus" in event.aliases
    assert event.shift
    assert event.ctrl


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "sequence,key,character,modifiers,base_key",
    [
        ("\x1b ", "alt+space", " ", ("alt",), "space"),
        ("\x1b\r", "alt+enter", "\r", ("alt",), "enter"),
        ("\x1b\x7f", "alt+backspace", "\x7f", ("alt",), "backspace"),
        ("\x1b\x01", "alt+ctrl+a", "\x01", ("alt", "ctrl"), "a"),
        ("\x1ba", "alt+a", "a", ("alt",), "a"),
        ("\x1bA", "alt+shift+a", "A", ("alt", "shift"), "a"),
    ],
)
def test_legacy_alt_prefixed_fallback(
    parser, sequence, key, character, modifiers, base_key
):
    """Legacy ESC-prefixed fallback keeps stable public keys and metadata."""
    events = list(parser.feed(sequence))
    events.extend(parser.feed(""))
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.key == key
    assert event.character == character
    assert event.modifiers == modifiers
    assert event.base_key == base_key

END STEP

@@ -272,6 +273,30 @@ KEY_DISPLAY_ALIASES = {
 
 ASCII_KEY_NAMES = {"\t": "tab"}
 
+# Alternate key names for keys which produce a different character when shifted.
+# The keys of this mapping are the Textual names of the shifted characters and
+# the values are the Textual names of the unshifted (base) keys. For example,
+# on a US layout pressing Shift+= produces "+", so "plus" is the shifted form
+# of "equals_sign".
+SHIFTED_KEY_NAMES: Final[dict[str, str]] = {
+    "underscore": "minus",
+    "plus": "equals_sign",
+    "left_curly_bracket": "left_square_bracket",
+    "right_curly_bracket": "right_square_bracket",
+    "vertical_line": "backslash",
+    "colon": "semicolon",
+    "quotation_mark": "apostrophe",
+    "less_than_sign": "comma",
+    "greater_than_sign": "full_stop",
+    "question_mark": "slash",
+    "tilde": "grave_accent",
+}
+
+# The inverse mapping, from the unshifted key name to its shifted name.
+BASE_TO_SHIFTED_KEYS: Final[dict[str, str]] = {
+    value: key for key, value in SHIFTED_KEY_NAMES.items()
+}
+
 
 def _get_unicode_name_from_key(key: str) -> str:
     """Get the best guess for the Unicode name of the char corresponding to the key.
@@ -282,8 +307,37 @@ def _get_unicode_name_from_key(key: str) -> str:
 
 
 def _get_key_aliases(key: str) -> list[str]:
-    """Return all aliases for the given key, including the key itself"""
-    return [key] + KEY_ALIASES.get(key, [])
+    """Return all aliases for the given key, including the key itself."""
+    aliases = [key] + list(KEY_ALIASES.get(key, []))
+    if "+" in key:
+        modifiers, key_name = key.rpartition("+")[::2]
+    else:
+        modifiers, key_name = "", key
+    modifier_tokens = modifiers.split("+") if modifiers else []
+    base_key = SHIFTED_KEY_NAMES.get(key_name)
+    if base_key is not None and "shift" not in modifier_tokens:
+        # The key name is a shifted form (e.g. "plus") which implies the shift
+        # modifier was held. Add an alias using the unshifted base key so that
+        # handlers/bindings declared with either form match.
+        if modifiers:
+            aliases.append(f"{modifiers}+shift+{base_key}")
+        else:
+            aliases.append(f"shift+{base_key}")
+    else:
+        shifted_key = BASE_TO_SHIFTED_KEYS.get(key_name)
+        if shifted_key is not None and "shift" in modifier_tokens:
+            # The key name is an unshifted base key (e.g. "equals_sign") with
+            # shift held. Add an alias using the shifted form, dropping the
+            # implicit shift modifier.
+            shifted_modifiers = "+".join(
+                token for token in modifier_tokens if token != "shift"
+            )
+            aliases.append(
+                f"{shifted_modifiers}+{shifted_key}"
+                if shifted_modifiers
+                else shifted_key
+            )
+    return aliases
 
 
 @lru_cache(1024)

STEP 187 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "Extend Key events with Kitty keyboard protocol metadata

Add phase, modifiers, base_key, shifted_key, and base_layout_key fields to
the Key event, along with convenience properties (is_press, is_repeat,
is_release, shift, alt, ctrl, super, hyper, meta). The XTerm parser now
handles Kitty keyboard protocol press/repeat/release event types and
text-reporting sequences, preserving stable metadata for printable and
alternate keys.

Alternate keys (e.g. Ctrl+Shift+=) now expose the shifted form as an alias
(ctrl+plus) and binding matching considers key aliases, so shifted-form
shortcuts match again. The legacy ESC-prefixed fallback preserves public key
names and metadata for Enter, Space, Backspace, and Ctrl+letter.

Add examples/kitty_keyboard_protocol.py demonstrating the new API."
